## Intr<sub>o</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n

Scale Drift. This efect is visible in Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 3 (b,c). Und<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e same exp</sub>l<sub>ana</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> EM <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>ema</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y s</sub>hift<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e mes-</sub> <sub>sage sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>owar</sub>d <sub>sma</sub>ll<sub>er va</sub>l<sub>ues compare</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> <sub>or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>grap</sub>h<sub>.</sub> A<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>messages</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>repea</sub>t<sub>-</sub> <sub>e</sub>dl<sub>y</sub> t<sub>rans</sub>f<sub>orme</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d <sub>propaga</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>across</sub> l<sub>ayers,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>per</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>b<sub>e</sub>d <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on gra</sub>d<sub>ua</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> d<sub>epar</sub>t<sub>s</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>propaga</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> regime. We term this progressive mismatch as Scale Drift.

L<sub>e</sub>t $\widetilde { S } _ { \mathrm { E M } } ^ { ( l ) } ( G )$ denotes the layer-l message scale of the EM-<sub>per</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>b<sub>e</sub>d <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> $\eta _ { e } ^ { ( l ) }$ <sub>as</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f layer-l scale carried by the message induced by edge e:

$$
\eta _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } : = \| m _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } / \sum _ { a \in \mathcal { E } } \| m _ { a } ^ { ( l ) } \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } .\tag{5}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ow</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>eorem</sub> f<sub>orma</sub>li<sub>zes</sub> EM<sub>-</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d S<sub>ca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> D<sub>r</sub>ift<sub>.</sub> Theorem 1 (Layer-wise Scale Contraction under EM). For any layer l, applying EM with coeficients $\{ \rho _ { e } \in$ [0, 1]} <sub>∈E</sub> yields

$$
\frac { S _ { \mathrm { E M } } ^ { ( l ) } ( G ) } { S ^ { ( l ) } ( G ) } \le 1 - \sum _ { e \in \mathcal { E } } ( 1 - \rho _ { e } ^ { 2 } ) \eta _ { e } ^ { ( l ) }\tag{6}
$$

The contraction is strict whenever there exists an edge e such that $\eta _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } > 0$ and $\rho _ { e } < 1$

Proof. See Appendix B. Theorem 1 shows that EM introduces <sub>a</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>c sca</sub>l<sub>e con</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>on w</sub>h<sub>enever a message w</sub>ith <sub>nonzero</sub> <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>suppresse</sub>d<sub>.</sub> R<sub>epea</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> <sub>across</sub> l<sub>ayers</sub> l<sub>ea</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ow</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th<sub>-</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>resu</sub>lt<sub>.</sub>

Corollary 2 (Depth-amplified Scale Drift under EM). Under the conditions of Theorem 1, suppose an edge e satisfies $\rho _ { e } \in ( 0 , 1 )$ and $\eta _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } \geq \underline { { { \eta } } } > 0 .$ for all l = 1, . . . , L. Then,

$$
- \sum _ { l = 1 } ^ { L } \log \frac { S _ { \mathrm { E M } } ^ { ( l ) } ( G ) } { S ^ { ( l ) } ( G ) } \geq - L \log \left[ 1 - ( 1 - \rho _ { e } ^ { 2 } ) \underline { { \eta } } \right] .\tag{7}
$$

Hence, the drift grows at least linearly with deeper layers. Proof. See Appendix B. Corollary 2 shows how repeated l<sub>ayer-w</sub>i<sub>se</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> <sub>accumu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith d<sub>ep</sub>th<sub>,</sub> <sub>prov</sub>idi<sub>ng</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>mec</sub>h<sub>an</sub>i<sub>sm-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>exp</sub>l<sub>ana</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th<sub>-amp</sub>lifi<sub>e</sub>d <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> discre<sub>p</sub>anc<sub>y</sub> observed in Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 3(d).

Th<sub>e</sub> di<sub>agnos</sub>i<sub>s a</sub>b<sub>ove sugges</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>a</sub> d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>ra</sub>bl<sub>e a</sub>lt<sub>erna</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ou</sub>ld <sub>per</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>b th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on carr</sub>i<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y e</sub>d<sub>ge-</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d <sub>mes-</sub> <sub>sages w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y s</sub>h<sub>r</sub>i<sub>n</sub>ki<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d in Eq. (2). To this end, NICE introduces Noise Corruption (NC), which replaces zero-directed suppression with <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d<sub>-norm</sub> <sub>ran</sub>d<sub>om-</sub>di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>corrup</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> F<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>mes-</sub> <sup>sa</sup>g<sup>e</sup> $\mathbf { m } _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { l } }$ induced by edge e at layer $l ,$ we sam<sub>p</sub><sup>l</sup>e a <sub>corrup</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>coun</sub>t<sub>erpar</sub>t f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e sp</sub>h<sub>ere w</sub>ith th<sub>e same norm:</sub>

$$
\epsilon _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } \sim \mathcal { P } _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } : = \mathrm { U n i f } \left( \left\{ \mathbf { z } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { l } } : \| \mathbf { z } \| _ { 2 } = \| \mathbf { m } _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } \| _ { 2 } \right\} \right)\tag{8}
$$

We <sub>us</sub>e $\mathcal { P } _ { \mathrm { N C } }$ to denote the joint distribution of all corru<sub>p</sub>te<sup>d</sup> counter<sub>p</sub>arts sam<sub>p</sub><sup>l</sup>e<sup>d</sup> across e<sup>d</sup><sub>g</sub>es an<sup>d</sup> messa<sub>g</sub>e-<sub>pass</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> l<sub>ayers</sub> i<sub>n one</sub> NC f<sub>orwar</sub>d <sub>pass.</sub> Gi<sub>ven a res</sub>t<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> g<sup>ate</sup> $\rho _ { e } \in [ 0 , 1 ]$ ], NC corrupts the message with the noise:

$$
\Psi _ { \mathrm { N C } , \rho _ { e } } ^ { ( l ) } \left( \mathbf { m } _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } \right) = \sqrt { \rho _ { e } } \mathbf { m } _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } + \sqrt { 1 - \rho _ { e } } \pmb { \epsilon } _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } .\tag{9}
$$

H<sub>ere,</sub> $\rho _ { e }$ <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>s</sub> h<sub>ow</sub> f<sub>ar</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>message</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>res</sub>t<sub>ore</sub>d t<sub>owar</sub>d it<sub>s c</sub>l<sub>ean</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>on:</sub> $\rho _ { e } = 0$ <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ves a</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll<sub>y corrup</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>coun</sub>t<sub>er-</sub> <sub>p</sub>art, w<sup>h</sup>ereas $\rho _ { e } = 1$ <sub>recovers</sub> th<sub>e c</sub>l<sub>ean message.</sub> Th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore,</sub> <sup>V</sup>ar<sub>y</sub><sup>i</sup>n<sub>g</sub> $\rho _ { e }$ d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>nes</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>con</sub>ti<sub>nuous</sub> <sub>res</sub>t<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>pa</sub>th <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d<sub>-norm corrup</sub>ti<sub>on space.</sub> Th<sub>e propos</sub>iti<sub>on s</sub>h<sub>ows</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e res</sub>t<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>on pa</sub>th <sub>sa</sub>ti<sub>s</sub>fi<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e sca</sub>l<sub>e-s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y requ</sub>i<sub>remen</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Proposition 3 (Scale-Stable Restoration Path under NC). For any fixed message $\mathbf { m } _ { e } ^ { ( l ) }$ and restoration gate $\rho _ { e } \in [ 0 , 1 ]$

$$
\mathbb { E } _ { \epsilon _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } } \left[ \left\| \Psi _ { \mathrm { N C } , \rho _ { e } } ^ { ( l ) } \left( \mathbf { m } _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } \right) \right\| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } \right] = \left\| \mathbf { m } _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } \right\| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } .\tag{10}
$$

Restoration bo<sub>u</sub>ndar<sub>y</sub> learnin<sub>g.</sub> SRB b<sub>a</sub>l<sub>a</sub>n<sub>ces s</sub>t<sub>oc</sub>h<sub>as</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> <sub>res</sub>t<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>on r</sub>i<sub>s</sub>k <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t <sub>res</sub>t<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>on compac</sub>t<sub>ness</sub> b<sub>y:</sub>

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { S R B } } ( \boldsymbol { \rho } ) = \mathcal { R } _ { y ^ { * } } ( \boldsymbol { \rho } ) + \lambda _ { \mathrm { r e s t } } \| \boldsymbol { \rho } \| _ { 1 } ,\tag{13}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $\lambda _ { \mathrm { { r e s t } } } \geq 0$ <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>d<sub>e-o</sub>f b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween res</sub>t<sub>or</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng a compac</sub>t <sub>res</sub>t<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>on con</sub>fi<sub>g-</sub> <sub>ura</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Th<sub>e resu</sub>lti<sub>ng s</sub>t<sub>oc</sub>h<sub>as</sub>ti<sub>c res</sub>t<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ary</sub> i<sub>s</sub>

To bridge this gap, we propose Boundary-Integrated Gradient (BIG), which integrates this contribution from the fully <sub>corrup</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>earne</sub>d b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ary</sub> <sub>a</sub>l<sub>ong:</sub>

$$
\pmb { \rho } ^ { \star } = \arg \operatorname* { m i n } _ { \pmb { \rho } \in [ 0 , 1 ] ^ { | \varepsilon | } } \mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { S R B } } ( \pmb { \rho } ) .\tag{16}
$$

$$
\rho ( t ) = t \rho ^ { \star } , \qquad t \in [ 0 , 1 ] .\tag{14}
$$

The attribution score of edge e is then computed as

I<sub>n prac</sub>ti<sub>ce, we es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>e</sub> $\mathcal { R } _ { y ^ { \ast } } ( \rho )$ using N independent NC <sub>sa</sub>m<sub>p</sub>l<sub>es.</sub> L<sub>e</sub>t $\epsilon ^ { ( n ) } \sim \mathcal { P } _ { \mathrm { N C } }$ <sub>an</sub>d $d _ { n } = d _ { y ^ { * } } ( \pmb { \rho } , \pmb { \epsilon } ^ { ( n ) } )$ f<sub>or</sub> $n = 1 , \ldots , N$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e emp</sub>i<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>res</sub>t<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>on r</sub>i<sub>s</sub>k i<sub>s</sub>

$$
s _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { B I G } } = - \rho _ { e } ^ { \star } \int _ { 0 } ^ { 1 } \left. \frac { \partial \mathcal { R } _ { y ^ { \ast } } ( \pmb { \rho } ) } { \partial \rho _ { e } } \right| _ { \pmb { \rho = \rho } ( t ) } \mathrm { d } t .\tag{17}
$$

$$
\widehat { \mathcal { R } } _ { y ^ { * } } ^ { ( N ) } ( \pmb { \rho } ) = \bar { d } _ { N } + \beta \sqrt { \frac { 1 } { N } \sum _ { n = 1 } ^ { N } ( d _ { n } - \bar { d } _ { N } ) ^ { 2 } } , \bar { d } _ { N } = \frac { 1 } { N } \sum _ { n = 1 } ^ { N } d _ { n } .\tag{15}
$$

Followin<sub>g p</sub>rior work (Wan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2023), we W<sub>e eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e on e</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>grap</sub>h<sub>-c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s w</sub>ith <sub>anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>exp</sub>l<sub>ana</sub>t<sub>ory su</sub>b<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ures,</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> f<sub>our w</sub>id<sub>e</sub>l<sub>y</sub> used molecular datasets namel<sub>y</sub> Muta<sub>g</sub>enicit<sub>y</sub> (M<sub>g</sub>), Benzene (Bz), Alkane-Carbon<sub>y</sub>l (AC) and Fluoride-Carbon<sub>y</sub>l (FC) (A<sub>g</sub>arwal et al. 2023). Additionall<sub>y</sub> include seven tasks from th<sub>e more c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>eng</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> B<sub>-</sub>XAIC b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>, name</sub>l<sub>y</sub> I<sub>n</sub>d<sub>o</sub>l<sub>e,</sub> PAINS, Rin<sub>g</sub>s-Count and Rin<sub>g</sub>s-Max (Proszewska, Danel, and R<sub>y</sub>marcz<sub>y</sub>k 2025). Details are <sub>p</sub>rovided in A<sub>pp</sub>endix C. Baselines. Three types of baselines are adopted. (1) Reference explainers: GTExplainer which returns the groundt<sub>ru</sub>th <sub>exp</sub>l<sub>ana</sub>ti<sub>ons as an orac</sub>l<sub>e re</sub>f<sub>erence an</sub>d R<sub>an</sub>d<sub>om ex-</sub> plains randomly as a weak reference. (2) White-box explainers: gradient-based methods including Saliency (Simon<sub>y</sub>an, Vedaldi, and Zisserman 2014) and GuidedBackprop (Springenberg et al. 2015). (3) Black-box explainers: methods follow the Perturb-Quer<sub>y</sub> paradi<sub>g</sub>m, includin<sub>g</sub> GN-NEx<sub>p</sub>lainer (Guo et al. 2023), PGEx<sub>p</sub>lainer (Burkart and Huber 2021), ReFine (Wan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2021), D4Ex<sub>p</sub>lainer (Chen et al. 2023), Prox<sub>y</sub>Ex<sub>p</sub>lainer (Chen et al. 2024), ConfEx-<sub>p</sub>lainer (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2025). More details see A<sub>pp</sub>endix D.

## Ablation Study (RQ3 & RQ4)

Why SRB is necessary. Table 3 verifies the dual objectives <sub>o</sub>f SRB<sub>.</sub> R<sub>emov</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on pena</sub>lt<sub>y</sub> i<sub>ncreases</sub> th<sub>e mean</sub> prediction degradation by 61% and its standard deviation by 217%, showing that variation aware optimization boosts <sub>res</sub>t<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>on qua</sub>lit<sub>y an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y across</sub> NC <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>es.</sub> With<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>compac</sub>t<sub>ness</sub> <sub>regu</sub>l<sub>ar</sub>i<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>res</sub>t<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>o</sub> i<sub>ncreases</sub> b<sub>y</sub> 211%, while AUC decreases by 6.9%. T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 6<sub>:</sub> Th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>cs o</sub>f th<sub>e eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>s an</sub>d th<sub>e sp</sub>lit<sub>.</sub>

Si<sub>nce</sub> $\textstyle \sum _ { a \in { \mathcal { E } } } \eta _ { a } ^ { ( l ) } = 1$ <sub>, we</sub> h<sub>ave</sub>

$$
\begin{array} { r l r } & { } & { \displaystyle \sum _ { a \in \mathcal { E } } \rho _ { a } ^ { 2 } \eta _ { a } ^ { ( l ) } = \sum _ { a \in \mathcal { E } } \left[ 1 - \left( 1 - \rho _ { a } ^ { 2 } \right) \right] \eta _ { a } ^ { ( l ) } } \\ & { } & { \quad = 1 - \displaystyle \sum _ { a \in \mathcal { E } } ( 1 - \rho _ { a } ^ { 2 } ) \eta _ { a } ^ { ( l ) } . } \end{array}\tag{23}
$$

B<sub>ecause</sub> $\rho _ { a } \in [ 0 , 1 ]$ <sub>an</sub>d $\eta _ { a } ^ { ( l ) } \geq 0$ , <sup>ever</sup>y <sup>term</sup> $( 1 - \rho _ { a } ^ { 2 } ) \eta _ { a } ^ { ( l ) }$ i<sub>s</sub> n<sub>o</sub>n<sub>-</sub>n<sub>ega</sub>ti<sub>ve.</sub> H<sub>e</sub>n<sub>ce,</sub>

$$
\frac { S _ { \mathrm { E M } } ^ { ( l ) } ( G ) } { S ^ { ( l ) } ( G ) } = 1 - \sum _ { a \in \mathcal { E } } ( 1 - \rho _ { a } ^ { 2 } ) \eta _ { a } ^ { ( l ) } \le 1 .\tag{24}
$$

M<sub>oreover,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>nequa</sub>lit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub>t <sub>w</sub>h<sub>enever</sub> th<sub>ere ex</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>s an</sub> edge a such that $\eta _ { a } ^ { ( l ) } > 0$ <sub>an</sub>d $\rho _ { a } ~ < ~ 1$ <sub>,</sub> b<sub>ecause</sub> th<sub>e cor-</sub> res<sub>p</sub>on<sup>di</sup>n<sub>g</sub> term $( 1 - \rho _ { a } ^ { 2 } ) \eta _ { a } ^ { ( l ) }$ i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>en s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub>tl<sub>y pos</sub>iti<sub>ve.</sub> Thi<sub>s</sub> <sub>comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>proo</sub>f<sub>.</sub> □

## Pr<sub>oo</sub>f <sub>o</sub>f C<sub>o</sub>r<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>a</sub>r<sub>y</sub> 2

Suppose that an edge e is masked with a fixed coeficient $\rho _ { e } \in ( 0 , 1 )$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>sa</sub>ti<sub>s</sub>fi<sub>es</sub>

$$
\eta _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } \ge \underline { { \eta } } > 0 , \qquad l = 1 , \ldots , L .\tag{25}
$$

Theorem 1 gives, for every layer l,

$$
\begin{array} { r l r } {  { \frac { S _ { \mathrm { E M } } ^ { ( l ) } ( G ) } { S ^ { ( l ) } ( G ) } = 1 - \sum _ { a \in \mathcal { E } } ( 1 - \rho _ { a } ^ { 2 } ) \eta _ { a } ^ { ( l ) } } } \\ & { } & \\ & { } & { \leq 1 - ( 1 - \rho _ { e } ^ { 2 } ) \eta _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } } \\ & { } & { \leq 1 - ( 1 - \rho _ { e } ^ { 2 } ) \eta . } \end{array}\tag{26}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t i<sub>nequa</sub>lit<sub>y</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ows</sub> b<sub>ecause a</sub>ll <sub>om</sub>itt<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>erms are</sub> <sub>non-nega</sub>ti<sub>ve.</sub> D<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>

$$
q = 1 - ( 1 - \rho _ { e } ^ { 2 } ) \underline { { \eta } } .\tag{27}
$$

Si<sub>nce</sub> $\rho _ { e } \in ( 0 , 1 )$ <sub>an</sub>d $\eta > 0 .$ <sub>, we</sub> h<sub>ave</sub> $0 < q < 1$ <sub>.</sub> B<sub>ecause</sub> the function − log x is monotonically decreasing on $( 0 , \infty )$ Eq. (26) im<sub>p</sub>lies

$$
- \log \frac { \mathcal { S } _ { \mathrm { E M } } ^ { ( l ) } ( G ) } { \mathcal { S } ^ { ( l ) } ( G ) } \geq - \log q .\tag{28}
$$

S<sub>umm</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e a</sub>b<sub>ove</sub> i<sub>nequa</sub>lit<sub>y over</sub> $l = 1 , \ldots , L$ <sub>y</sub>i<sub>e</sub>ld<sub>s</sub>

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { E M } } ^ { ( L ) } = - \displaystyle \sum _ { l = 1 } ^ { L } \log \frac { S _ { \mathrm { E M } } ^ { ( l ) } ( G ) } { S ^ { ( l ) } ( G ) } } \\ & { ~ \geq - L \log q } \\ & { ~ = - L \log \left[ 1 - ( 1 - \rho _ { e } ^ { 2 } ) \eta \right] . } \end{array}\tag{29}
$$

The right-hand side is linear in L with a strictly positive <sub>coe</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t b<sub>ecause</sub> $q ~ \in ~ ( 0 , 1 )$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore,</sub> th<sub>e cumu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> l<sub>og-sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> d<sub>r</sub>ift <sub>grows a</sub>t l<sub>eas</sub>t li<sub>near</sub>l<sub>y w</sub>ith th<sub>e num</sub>b<sub>er o</sub>f <sup>messa</sup>g<sup>e-</sup>p<sup>assin</sup>g <sup>la</sup>y<sup>ers</sup>. □

## Pr<sub>oo</sub>f <sub>o</sub>f Pr<sub>opos</sub>iti<sub>o</sub>n 3

F<sub>or</sub> b<sub>rev</sub>it<sub>y,</sub> l<sub>e</sub>t $\mathbf { m } = \mathbf { m } _ { e } ^ { ( l ) } , \epsilon = \epsilon _ { e } ^ { ( l ) }$ <sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d $\rho = \rho _ { e }$ <sub>.</sub> B<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>n</sub>iti<sub>on o</sub>f NC<sub>,</sub>

$$
\Psi _ { \mathrm { N C } , \rho } ^ { ( l ) } ( \mathbf { m } ) = \sqrt { \rho } \mathbf { m } + \sqrt { 1 - \rho } \epsilon .\tag{30}
$$

E<sub>xpan</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> it<sub>s</sub> <sub>square</sub>d <sub>norm</sub> <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub>

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left\| \Psi _ { \mathrm { N C } , \rho } ^ { ( l ) } ( \mathbf { m } ) \right\| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } = \rho \| \mathbf { m } \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } + ( 1 - \rho ) \| \pmb { \epsilon } \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } } \\ { + 2 \sqrt { \rho ( 1 - \rho ) } \mathbf { m } ^ { \top } \pmb { \epsilon } . } \end{array}\tag{31}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>corrup</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>coun</sub>t<sub>erpar</sub>t i<sub>s</sub> <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d <sub>un</sub>if<sub>orm</sub>l<sub>y</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> s<sub>p</sub><sup>h</sup>ere

$$
\left\{ \mathbf { z } : \| \mathbf { z } \| _ { 2 } = \| \mathbf { m } \| _ { 2 } \right\} .\tag{32}
$$

Th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore,</sub>

$$
\| \epsilon \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } = \| \mathbf { m } \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 }\tag{33}
$$

<sub>a</sub>l<sub>mos</sub>t <sub>sure</sub>l<sub>y.</sub> M<sub>oreover,</sub> th<sub>e un</sub>if<sub>orm</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on on a sp</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> i<sub>s symme</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c aroun</sub>d th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>n, an</sub>d h<sub>ence</sub>

$$
\mathbb { E } _ { \epsilon } [ \epsilon ] = \mathbf { 0 } .\tag{34}
$$

<sup>C</sup>onse<sub>q</sub>uent<sup>l</sup><sub>y</sub>,

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathbb { E } _ { \epsilon } [ \mathbf { m } ^ { \top } \epsilon ] = \mathbf { m } ^ { \top } \mathbb { E } _ { \epsilon } [ \epsilon ] = 0 . } \end{array}\tag{35}
$$

Takin<sub>g</sub> ex<sub>p</sub>ectation on both sides of Eq. (31), we obtain

$$
\begin{array} { r l r } & { } & { \mathbb { E } _ { \epsilon } \left[ \left\| \Psi _ { \mathrm { N C } , \rho } ^ { ( l ) } ( \mathbf { m } ) \right\| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } \right] = \rho \| \mathbf { m } \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } + ( 1 - \rho ) \| \mathbf { m } \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } } \\ & { } & { = \| \mathbf { m } \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } . } \end{array}\tag{36}
$$

## M<sub>o</sub>l<sub>ecu</sub>l<sub>ar</sub> E<sub>xp</sub>l<sub>ana</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> B<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s</sub>

Mutagenicity. Muta<sub>g</sub>enicit<sub>y</sub> (Kazius, McGuire, and Bursi 2005) contains 4,337 molecular graphs labeled according t<sub>o w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e correspon</sub>di<sub>ng compoun</sub>d <sub>ex</sub>hibit<sub>s mu</sub>t<sub>agen</sub>i<sub>c</sub> <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>v</sub>it<sub>y.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>n</sub>it<sub>ro</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>am</sub>i<sub>no</sub> f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>groups</sub> $\mathrm { ( N O _ { 2 } }$ and NH<sub>2</sub>) serve as the ground-truth explanations. The dataset contains on average 30.32 nodes and 30.77 edges per g<sup>ra</sup>p<sup>h</sup>.

Benzene. Benzene (A<sub>g</sub>arwal et al. 2023) is a binar<sub>y</sub> classification dataset containing 12,000 molecular graphs sampled f<sub>rom</sub> ZINC15<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>ne w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er a mo</sub>l<sub>ecu</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns a</sub>t l<sub>eas</sub>t <sub>one</sub> b<sub>enzene r</sub>i<sub>ng.</sub> Th<sub>e a</sub>t<sub>oms an</sub>d b<sub>on</sub>d<sub>s</sub> f<sub>orm</sub>i<sub>ng eac</sub>h d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>enzene r</sub>i<sub>ng are anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>groun</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ru</sub>th <sub>exp</sub>l<sub>ana</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Wh<sub>en</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>mo</sub>l<sub>ecu</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>-</sub> ple disjoint benzene rings, each ring is regarded as a valid explanatory structure. The graphs contain on average 20.58 nodes and 43.65 edges.

Alkane-Carbonyl. Alkane-Carbon<sub>y</sub>l (A<sub>g</sub>arwal et al. 2023) contains 4,326 molecular graphs. Mixu<sub>p</sub>-based methods <sub>com</sub>bi<sub>ne exp</sub>l<sub>ana</sub>t<sub>ory s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ures w</sub>ith b<sub>ase grap</sub>h<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o re</sub>d<sub>uce</sub> th<sub>e</sub> di<sub>screpancy</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween</sub> <sub>exp</sub>l<sub>ana</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>su</sub>b<sub>grap</sub>h<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> <sub>or</sub>i<sub>g-</sub> inal data distribution (Zhan , Luo, and Wei 2023). O<sub>nce</sub> SRB t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>s comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e,</sub> th<sub>e exp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ner parame</sub>t<sub>ers an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t GNN <sub>are</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th fi<sub>xe</sub>d<sub>.</sub> F<sub>or eac</sub>h <sub>grap</sub>h<sub>, we</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t <sub>com-</sub> <sub>pu</sub>t<sub>e</sub> it<sub>s res</sub>t<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ary</sub> $\rho ^ { \star }$ <sub>a</sub>nd th<sub>e</sub>n <sub>use</sub> BIG t<sub>o co</sub>n<sub>ve</sub>rt the joint restoration process into edge-level attributions.

Representation distance. Let h(G) denote the clean <sub>g</sub>r<sub>ap</sub>h r<sub>ep</sub>r<sub>ese</sub>nt<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n<sub>, o</sub>bt<sub>a</sub>in<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y app</sub>l<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>a</sub>r<sub>ge</sub>t GNN’<sub>s</sub> <sub>rea</sub>d<sub>ou</sub>t f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l<sub>-</sub>l<sub>ayer no</sub>d<sub>e represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> L<sub>e</sub>t $\widetilde { \mathbf { h } } _ { \Psi } ( G ; \rho , \epsilon )$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e correspon</sub>di<sub>ng represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> <sub>per</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>b<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on pr</sub>i<sub>m</sub>iti<sub>ve</sub> $\Psi \in \{ \mathrm { E } \mathrm { \bar { M } } , \mathrm { N C } \}$ <sub>.</sub> W<sub>e measure</sub> th<sub>e nor-</sub> <sub>ma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ance</sub> b<sub>y</sub>

$$
D _ { \mathrm { r e p r } } = \frac { \left\| \widetilde { \mathbf { h } } _ { \Psi } - \mathbf { h } ( G ) \right\| _ { 2 } } { \left\| \mathbf { h } ( G ) \right\| _ { 2 } + \varepsilon } ,\tag{49}
$$

where ε is a small constant for numerical stability. For $\mathrm { N C } ,$ Eq. (49) is avera<sub>g</sub>ed over the inde<sub>p</sub>endent corru<sub>p</sub>tion sam<sub>p</sub>les. $\mathbf { A }$ <sub>sma</sub>ll<sub>er va</sub>l<sub>ue</sub> i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e per</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>b<sub>e</sub>d <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>ns c</sub>l<sub>oser</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e c</sub>l<sub>ean grap</sub>h <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Tar<sub>g</sub>et-<sub>p</sub>rediction de<sub>g</sub>radation<sub>.</sub> L<sub>e</sub>t

$$
y ^ { * } = \arg \operatorname* { m a x } _ { c } p _ { c } ( G )\tag{50}
$$

b<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> W<sub>e measure</sub> th<sub>e one-s</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d d<sub>egra</sub>d<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f it<sub>s</sub> l<sub>og-pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y as</sub>

$$
D _ { \mathrm { p r e d } } = \left[ \log p _ { y ^ { \ast } } ( G ) - \log p _ { y ^ { \ast } } \left( \pmb { \rho } , \pmb { \epsilon } \right) \right] _ { + } .\tag{51}
$$

Th<sub>e me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> i<sub>s zero w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> th<sub>e per</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>b<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on preserves or</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>reng</sub>th<sub>ens</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t<sub>-c</sub>l<sub>ass pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y, an</sub>d i<sub>ncreases on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en suppor</sub>t f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> d<sub>ecreases.</sub> F<sub>or</sub> $\mathrm { N } \dot { \mathrm { C } }$ th<sub>e repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>va</sub>l<sub>ue</sub> i<sub>s average</sub>d <sub>over</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>corrup-</sub> ti<sub>on samp</sub>l<sub>es.</sub> L<sub>ower va</sub>l<sub>ues are</sub> b<sub>e</sub>tt<sub>er</sub> f<sub>or</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs.</sub>

F<sub>or</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> $D ,$ th<sub>e</sub> <sub>re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> i<sub>mprovemen</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f NC <sub>over</sub> EM i<sub>s</sub> <sub>co</sub>m<sub>pu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub>

$$
\mathrm { I m p . } = \frac { D _ { \mathrm { E M } } - D _ { \mathrm { N C } } } { D _ { \mathrm { E M } } } \times 1 0 0 \% .\tag{52}
